### What exists today (to be generalised)
The folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:
- `Dockerfile` — Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git
- `entrypoint.sh` — idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases→codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver
- `healthserver.py` — tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck
- `railway.toml` — build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy
- `chain.sh` — thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`
- `chain.yaml.example` — chain spec format
- `README.md` — recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands

Hardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases→codex routing, `standard` robustness. Bug to fix: Claude Code CLI is installed in the image but the entrypoint never authenticates it — add an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auth path.

### Sprint 1 deliverables
1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:
   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.
   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency — confirm/add.
   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.
   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this — `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).

2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:
   - `megaplan cloud init` — scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root
   - `megaplan cloud build` — docker build the image
   - `megaplan cloud deploy` — render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`
   - `megaplan cloud status` — invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser — reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)
   - `megaplan cloud attach` — attach to tmux `agent` session
   - `megaplan cloud logs` — tail remote logs
   - `megaplan cloud exec "<cmd>"` — run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)
   - `megaplan cloud resume` — read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`
   - `megaplan cloud down` — pause without deleting volume

3. Documentation at `docs/cloud.md`: env-var / cloud.yaml reference, symptom→cause→fix table (port from existing README), secret-rotation and cost-monitoring pointers.

Reigh parity validation (success criterion, not a test to automate): after implementation, the existing `reigh-megaplan-dev` behavior can be reproduced by `megaplan cloud deploy` with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars. The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: `megaplan cloud` — Sprint 1 (provider-agnostic Railway runner)

## Overview

Goal: introduce a new `megaplan/cloud/` subpackage that generalises the working-but-hardcoded Railway deployment at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` into a reusable, spec-driven cloud runner exposed through the existing `megaplan` CLI. Sprint 1 ships only the Railway provider; the abstraction is built so sprint 2 can drop in fly/ssh/local providers without churn.

Repo shape (relevant): the `megaplan` package uses argparse subcommands wired in `megaplan/cli.py` (`build_parser`, `cli.py:826`), with multi-action subcommands defined per-module via `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)` (see `megaplan/chain.py:528` and dispatch at `megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`). Package data is loaded with `importlib.resources.files("megaplan")` (`megaplan/cli.py:600`). Hatchling packages the whole `megaplan/` tree, so any new resource files (Dockerfile, entrypoint, wrappers, templates) ship automatically. PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`).

Constraints that drove design choices:
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable.
- Idempotency contract of the boot script must be preserved (clone-if-missing, codex config always overwritten, megaplan always upgraded).
- Codex `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` retained with explanatory comment.
- `MEGAPLAN_REF` pin surfaced as `megaplan.ref` (default `main`).
- Adding `cloud` must not break any existing command.
- Reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching today's reigh env vars must be able to reproduce the existing deployment.

## Phase 1: Reconcile Source & Scaffold Subpackage

### Step 1: Read the existing reigh deployment (`~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/*`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** every file in `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`: `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`, `healthserver.py`, `railway.toml`, `chain.sh`, `chain.yaml.example`, `README.md`. (The planning agent did not have read access to that path; the executor must.)
2. **Catalogue** every hardcoded value (repo URL, workspace path, model, reasoning, email, agent routing, robustness) and every environment variable referenced by `entrypoint.sh`. Map each to a `cloud.yaml` field per the brief's schema.
3. **Note** the exact mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredoc bodies — these become standalone files in step 3.
4. **Identify** the exact `claude` CLI auth invocation gap (the brief says "investigate `claude setup-token` or similar"). Test what the installed `claude` CLI exposes for non-interactive auth and pick the simplest path. Document it in `docs/cloud.md`.
5. **Reconcile** any divergence between the brief's described schema and reality before writing code; update the spec schema in step 4 if needed.

### Step 2: Scaffold the subpackage layout (`megaplan/cloud/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the directory tree:
   ```
   megaplan/cloud/
     __init__.py
     cli.py              # build_cloud_parser + run_cloud_cli
     spec.py             # cloud.yaml dataclasses + loader/validator
     template.py         # entrypoint renderer
     providers/
       __init__.py
       base.py           # Provider ABC
       railway.py        # Railway implementation
     templates/
       Dockerfile
       entrypoint.sh.tmpl
       healthserver.py
       railway.toml.tmpl
       cloud.yaml.tmpl
     wrappers/
       mp-run
       mp-supervise
       mp-heartbeat
   ```
2. **Confirm** hatchling packages the non-`.py` resources by default. If `pyproject.toml` needs a `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]` block (e.g. for files without a `.py` extension), add it; otherwise leave it.
3. **Verify** by building the wheel locally (`./.venv/bin/python -m build --wheel`) and inspecting the wheel contents to confirm `templates/` and `wrappers/` are present.

### Step 3: Port resources verbatim, then parameterise (`megaplan/cloud/templates/`, `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Copy** `Dockerfile`, `healthserver.py`, and `railway.toml` from reigh into `megaplan/cloud/templates/` with no behavioural changes. The Dockerfile stays generic — it bakes the toolchain, not the per-deploy config.
2. **Copy** `entrypoint.sh` to `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl` and replace every hardcoded value with a Python `string.Template`-style placeholder (`${REPO_URL}`, `${WORKSPACE_PATH}`, `${CODEX_MODEL}`, `${CODEX_REASONING}`, `${CODEX_EMAIL}`, `${MEGAPLAN_REF}`, `${ROBUSTNESS}`, `${MODE}`, `${IDEA_FILE}`, `${CHAIN_SPEC}`, `${PHASE_AGENT_BLOCK}`, etc.). Keep the explanatory comment for `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`.
3. **Extract** the mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredocs from the original `entrypoint.sh` into standalone files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`. Replace the heredocs in the template with a `cp ${WRAPPERS_DIR}/mp-* /usr/local/bin/ && chmod +x ...` block. The wrappers must be installed into the image at build time (add a `COPY` step to the Dockerfile placing them at a known path).
4. **Add** a non-interactive Claude auth block to `entrypoint.sh.tmpl`: `if [ -n "${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:-}" ]; then <whatever step 1 found>; fi`. Place it next to the existing codex auth block.
5. **Add** a `cloud.yaml.tmpl` with sensible defaults filled in and inline comments explaining each section.

### Step 4: Spec loader (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** dataclasses mirroring the brief's schema:
   ```python
   @dataclass
   class RepoSpec: url: str; branch: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class CodexSpec: model: str; reasoning: str = "high"
   @dataclass
   class AutoSpec: idea_file: str; robustness: str = "standard"
   @dataclass
   class ChainSpec: spec: str  # path
   @dataclass
   class ResourcesSpec: volume: str | None = None; port: int = 8080
   @dataclass
   class MegaplanSpec: ref: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class CloudSpec:
       provider: str
       repo: RepoSpec
       agents: dict[str, str]   # {"default": "codex", optional per-phase overrides}
       codex: CodexSpec
       mode: str                # auto | chain | idle
       megaplan: MegaplanSpec
       resources: ResourcesSpec
       secrets: list[str]
       auto: AutoSpec | None = None
       chain: ChainSpec | None = None
   ```
2. **Implement** `load_spec(path: Path) -> CloudSpec` — open, `yaml.safe_load`, validate, raise `CliError("invalid_spec", ...)` on every missing/malformed field. Mirror the precision of `megaplan/chain.py:71-100`.
3. **Validate** mode-specific requirements: `mode=auto` ⇒ `auto.idea_file` required; `mode=chain` ⇒ `chain.spec` required; `mode=idle` ⇒ neither required.
4. **Validate** `agents.default` is in a known set (e.g. `{"claude", "codex", "hermes"}`) and per-phase keys are known phase names.
5. **Validate** `provider` against the known set — for sprint 1 only `"railway"` is permitted; reject others with a `CliError` saying "sprint 2 will add: fly, ssh, local".

### Step 5: Template renderer (`megaplan/cloud/template.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `render_entrypoint(spec: CloudSpec) -> str` using `string.Template.safe_substitute`. Source the `.tmpl` from package resources via `importlib.resources.files("megaplan.cloud").joinpath("templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl").read_text(...)`.
2. **Build** the per-phase agent override block as a shell snippet (e.g. `megaplan config set phase_agent.<phase> <agent>` lines) when `agents` has entries beyond `default`.
3. **Implement** `render_railway_toml(spec)` and `materialize_deploy_dir(spec, dest: Path)` that copies the (untemplated) Dockerfile, healthserver, wrappers, and the rendered entrypoint + railway.toml into a deploy staging dir.
4. **Single source of truth:** the renderer must NOT duplicate placeholder names — define them once at module top so tests can iterate over them.

## Phase 2: Provider Layer

### Step 6: Provider abstract base (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Define** an `abc.ABC` `Provider` with the methods sprint-1 commands need:
   ```python
   class Provider(ABC):
       def build(self, deploy_dir: Path) -> int: ...
       def deploy(self, deploy_dir: Path, *, secrets: dict[str, str]) -> int: ...
       def ssh_exec(self, command: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess: ...
       def attach(self, session: str = "agent") -> int: ...
       def logs(self, *, follow: bool = True) -> int: ...
       def status(self) -> dict: ...
       def down(self) -> int: ...
       def destroy(self) -> int: ...
   ```
2. **Document** in the docstring that sprint 2 will add `init_plan(self, ...)` and that providers must remain stateless beyond CLI/credential discovery.
3. **Provide** a small registry helper `get_provider(name: str, spec: CloudSpec) -> Provider` so `cli.py` doesn't import each backend directly.

### Step 7: Railway provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** each ABC method as a thin wrapper around `railway` CLI invocations: `railway up --detach --ci`, `railway down`, `railway ssh ...`, `railway variables --set k=v` (one per secret), `railway logs`, `railway status --json`.
2. **Detect** the `railway` binary up front; if missing, raise a `CliError` with code `provider_unavailable` and a one-line install hint pointing at https://docs.railway.app/develop/cli.
3. **`status()`** runs `railway ssh "./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>"` (or `cd /workspace/<repo> && megaplan status`) and returns the parsed JSON. Do not reinvent a parser — `megaplan status` already emits structured JSON via `handle_status` (`cli.py:353`). The plan name comes from a `--plan` arg on `cloud status` or an active-plan lookup.
4. **`deploy()`** uploads secrets via `railway variables --set` for each name in `spec.secrets`, sourcing values from the local environment. If a named secret is missing locally, error early with a list of missing names. (No silent skipping — secrets matter.)
5. **No retry/backoff logic** beyond what `railway` already does. Surface non-zero exits as `CliError("provider_failed", stderr)`.

## Phase 3: CLI Wiring

### Step 8: Cloud CLI module (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` modelled on `megaplan/chain.py:528-555`. Top-level `cloud` parser with subparsers: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. All accept an optional `--cloud-yaml PATH` (defaults to `<root>/cloud.yaml`).
2. **Implement** `run_cloud_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int` that dispatches to per-action functions. Each action loads the spec (except `init`), constructs a provider via `get_provider`, and calls one method.
3. **Per-action behaviours:**
   - **`init`** — copies `cloud.yaml.tmpl` to `<root>/cloud.yaml`. Refuses to overwrite without `--force`.
   - **`build`** — renders deploy dir, calls `provider.build(deploy_dir)`.
   - **`deploy`** — renders deploy dir, gathers secret values from env, calls `provider.deploy(...)`.
   - **`status`** — calls `provider.status()`, prints the JSON it returns. The reviewer/user can pipe to `jq`.
   - **`attach`** — calls `provider.attach("agent")`. Allow `--session` override (default `agent`, also accepts `heartbeat`).
   - **`logs`** — calls `provider.logs(follow=True)`. `--no-follow` for one-shot.
   - **`exec "<cmd>"`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(cmd)`, streams stdout/stderr, returns the remote exit code.
   - **`resume`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec("megaplan status --plan ...")`, parses `next_step` from the JSON, then `ssh_exec`s the corresponding command. Reuse the `next_step` shape `megaplan auto` already consumes (see `megaplan/auto.py`).
   - **`down`** — calls `provider.down()`.
   - **`destroy`** — calls `provider.destroy()`. Prompts for confirmation unless `--yes` is passed.
4. **Errors:** all `CliError`s flow through `_emit_error` (mirror `megaplan/chain.py:594-597`) so output is consistent with other megaplan commands.

### Step 9: Wire into top-level CLI (`megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Register** the parser inside `build_parser()` next to the existing `build_chain_parser` registration (`megaplan/cli.py:997-998`):
   ```python
   from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser
   build_cloud_parser(subparsers)
   ```
2. **Dispatch** in `main()` next to the `chain` block (`megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`):
   ```python
   if args.command == "cloud":
       from megaplan.cloud.cli import run_cloud_cli
       try:
           return run_cloud_cli(root, args)
       except CliError as error:
           return error_response(error, root=root)
   ```
3. **Do NOT** add `"cloud"` to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` — it has its own dispatch path, like `auto`/`chain`/`tiebreaker`.
4. **Keep** all imports lazy/scoped to avoid a perf hit on commands that don't touch cloud.

## Phase 4: Tests, Docs, Smoke Build

### Step 10: Unit tests for spec & renderer (`tests/test_cloud_spec.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_cloud_spec.py`** — happy-path load for each `mode`, plus rejections: missing repo.url, unknown provider, mode=auto without idea_file, mode=chain without chain.spec, unknown agent in `agents.default`, malformed `secrets` list. Mirror `tests/test_chain.py:50-100` style.
2. **`test_cloud_template.py`** — render with a fully-populated spec and assert (a) every documented placeholder is replaced, (b) no original hardcoded reigh string remains (`banodoco/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`), (c) the `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` line is preserved, (d) the Claude auth block is present when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in the secrets list, (e) per-phase agent overrides materialise into the expected `megaplan config set` lines.
3. **Iterate** over the placeholder constants exported from `template.py` to drive the "no placeholders left unfilled" assertion — guards against new placeholders being added without test coverage.

### Step 11: Unit test for the Railway provider with mocked subprocess (`tests/test_cloud_railway.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Patch** `subprocess.run` and assert the exact `railway ...` argv produced by each provider method.
2. **Cover** `build`, `deploy` (including secret upload sequence), `status` (returns parsed JSON from a mocked stdout), `down`, `destroy`, `ssh_exec`, `attach`, `logs`.
3. **Cover** the missing-binary path: monkeypatch `shutil.which("railway")` to `None` and assert `CliError("provider_unavailable", ...)`.
4. **Cover** the missing-secret path: spec lists secret `FOO` but `os.environ` doesn't have it → `CliError` with a list of missing names.

### Step 12: Smoke test for docker build (`tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Skip** at module import time if `shutil.which("docker") is None` with a clear `pytest.skip("docker not available; install Docker to run cloud build smoke test")`.
2. **Materialise** a fully-rendered deploy dir into `tmp_path`, then run `docker build --pull=false -t megaplan-cloud-smoke .` against it. Fail the test on non-zero exit.
3. **Tag** with `@pytest.mark.slow` so CI can opt in/out.

### Step 13: User docs (`docs/cloud.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/cloud.md` (the directory does not exist yet — create it). Sections: quick start, `cloud.yaml` field reference, env vars, secret-rotation pointer, cost-monitoring pointer (link to Railway dashboard), and the symptom→cause→fix table ported from the reigh README.
2. **Document** the exact non-interactive Claude auth path discovered in step 1.
3. **Document** the `railway` CLI install link and the failure mode if it's missing.
4. **Brief mention** that sprint 2 will add fly/ssh/local providers and `cloud init-plan` — set expectations, do not over-promise.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 first — every later decision (placeholder names, env vars, claude auth, wrapper script bodies) depends on what's actually in reigh.
2. Steps 2–3 lay down resources before any code references them.
3. Steps 4–5 (spec + renderer) before steps 6–7 (providers depend on the spec dataclasses).
4. Steps 8–9 (CLI wiring) after the providers exist.
5. Tests (10–12) run alongside or after each implementation step they cover; do not save them all for the end.
6. Docs (13) last so they reflect the shipped behaviour.

## Validation Order
1. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_template.py -x` (cheapest, no external deps).
2. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_railway.py -x` (mocked subprocess, still cheap).
3. Run the full suite: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -x` to confirm no regression in existing megaplan tests.
4. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud init` in a scratch dir; confirm `cloud.yaml` is written.
5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud build` against the scratch `cloud.yaml`; confirm a Docker image is produced (skip if no docker).
6. Manual: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud deploy` against a throwaway Railway project mirroring reigh's env vars; confirm the container boots, codex+claude both authenticate, megaplan installs at the pinned ref, tmux sessions start, healthserver answers on `:8080`. Document this run as the parity-validation success criterion artifact.

      "criterion": "Rendered entrypoint preserves `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"` in the codex config block with an explanatory comment.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests",
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Rendered entrypoint contains a non-interactive Claude auth block guarded by `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests",
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Railway provider unit tests with mocked subprocess pass and assert exact `railway ...` argv for each provider method.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Railway provider raises `CliError(\"provider_unavailable\", ...)` with an install-link message when the `railway` binary is missing.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Railway provider raises a `CliError` listing missing secret names when `spec.secrets` references env vars that are not set locally.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "All ten new subcommands (`init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`) are reachable via `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud --help`.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_shell"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan --help` and a representative existing subcommand (e.g. `megaplan list`) still work after the wiring change (regression guard).",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_shell"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-run`, `mp-supervise`, `mp-heartbeat` are standalone files (not heredocs inside `entrypoint.sh.tmpl`).",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan.ref` field in `cloud.yaml` defaults to `main` and is honored by the rendered entrypoint's megaplan install/upgrade step.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "read_files",
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`provider` field rejects anything other than `\"railway\"` in sprint 1 with a `CliError` mentioning the sprint-2 providers.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`docs/cloud.md` exists and documents: cloud.yaml field reference, env vars, the resolved Claude non-interactive auth path, the symptom\u2192cause\u2192fix table, and the `railway` CLI install requirement.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Built wheel (or sdist) contains `megaplan/cloud/templates/Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh.tmpl`, `healthserver.py`, and the three wrapper scripts.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "run_shell",
        "read_build_output"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Docker build smoke test passes when run on a host with docker available; skips cleanly with a clear message when docker is absent.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests",
        "run_shell"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "No file in the new subpackage exceeds ~400 lines; each module has a single clear responsibility (spec, template, provider).",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "End-to-end reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars deploys successfully via `megaplan cloud deploy` and reproduces the existing reigh-megaplan-dev container behavior (codex+claude auth, megaplan upgrade, tmux sessions, healthserver responsive).",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "subjective_judgment",
        "verify_physical_device"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Reigh-parity validation runbook is published (deferred to follow-up but referenced from `docs/cloud.md`).",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`) so no dependency change is needed.",
    "Hatchling's default file inclusion is sufficient to package `megaplan/cloud/templates/*` and `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/*` since they live under the `megaplan` package; if a `force-include` block turns out to be required, the executor will add it during step 2.",
    "The `railway` CLI is treated as a runtime dependency, not vendored. Missing-binary surfaces a `CliError` with a doc link rather than auto-installing.",
    "`cloud.yaml` lives at the project root (alongside `.megaplan/`); `megaplan cloud init` writes it there.",
    "Secret values are sourced from the local environment at deploy time using the names listed in `spec.secrets`; missing values fail fast with a list of names rather than silently skipping.",
    "`cloud status` reuses `megaplan status` JSON verbatim \u2014 no new parser is written.",
    "Provider plugin discovery for sprint 1 is a static dict (`{\"railway\": RailwayProvider}`); entry-point-based discovery is deferred to sprint 2 when more providers exist.",
    "`cloud destroy` requires explicit confirmation (interactive prompt unless `--yes`) since it removes the volume.",
    "The placeholder syntax in `entrypoint.sh.tmpl` is `${NAME}` (Python `string.Template`) rather than Jinja, to keep dependencies minimal and the template directly readable as shell.",
    "The Dockerfile stays generic \u2014 it bakes the toolchain only. Per-deploy config flows through the rendered entrypoint and Railway env vars, not through image rebuilds."
  ],
  "structure_warnings": []
}

      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",

        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.

> DEVELOPER

You are already running inside the megaplan harness for this step. Do the requested planning/review/execution work directly. Do NOT invoke the `megaplan` CLI, do NOT read or activate the `megaplan` skill, do NOT start nested megaplan plans, and do NOT recurse into another planning harness. You are an independent reviewer. Critique the plan against the actual repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan





        Idea:
Build `megaplan cloud` — a new subpackage at `megaplan/cloud/` that provides a built-in, provider-agnostic cloud runner wired into the existing megaplan CLI. This is sprint 1 of a two-sprint effort; the goal here is operational parity with the existing Railway setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`, nothing more.

### What exists today (to be generalised)
The folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:
- `Dockerfile` — Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git
- `entrypoint.sh` — idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases→codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver
- `healthserver.py` — tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck
- `railway.toml` — build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy
- `chain.sh` — thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`
- `chain.yaml.example` — chain spec format
- `README.md` — recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands

Hardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases→codex routing, `standard` robustness. ### Sprint 1 deliverables
1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:
   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.
   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency — confirm/add.
   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.
   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this — `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).
   - Railway provider plugin: thin wrapper over `railway` CLI (`up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables --set`, `logs`, `status`). Live at `megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py` with a `Provider` abstract base at `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py` ready for sprint-2 plugins.

2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:
   - `megaplan cloud init` — scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root
   - `megaplan cloud build` — docker build the image
   - `megaplan cloud deploy` — render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`
   - `megaplan cloud status` — invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser — reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)
   - `megaplan cloud attach` — attach to tmux `agent` session
   - `megaplan cloud logs` — tail remote logs
   - `megaplan cloud exec "<cmd>"` — run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)
   - `megaplan cloud resume` — read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`
   - `megaplan cloud down` — pause without deleting volume
   - `megaplan cloud destroy` — full teardown

3. Documentation at `docs/cloud.md`: env-var / cloud.yaml reference, symptom→cause→fix table (port from existing README), secret-rotation and cost-monitoring pointers.

4. Tests:
   - Unit: spec loader (happy path + validation errors)
   - Unit: template renderer (all hardcoded strings correctly replaced)
   - Unit: Railway provider with mocked subprocess
   - Smoke: `docker build` on the generated Dockerfile must succeed (CI-runnable; can be skipped on systems without docker with a clear warning)

5. Reigh parity validation (success criterion, not a test to automate): after implementation, the existing `reigh-megaplan-dev` behavior can be reproduced by `megaplan cloud deploy` with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars. The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.

- Do NOT ship `cloud init-plan` bootstrap — sprint 2.
- Do NOT add a `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` — sprint 2.
- Do NOT retire `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` — it keeps running in parallel until sprint 2 migrates reigh fully.

- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable: if sprint 2 slips, this release is still useful on its own.
- Preserve the idempotency contract of the current entrypoint (repo clone only if missing; codex config always overwritten on boot; megaplan always upgraded on boot).
- Preserve `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` in the generated codex config with an explanatory comment — the container IS the sandbox.
- Preserve the `MEGAPLAN_REF` pinning mechanism, now surfaced as `megaplan.ref` in `cloud.yaml` (defaults to `main`).
- `railway` CLI is an acceptable runtime dependency; document it and fail gracefully with a link if missing.
- Do not break existing megaplan commands — `cloud` is additive.

        # Implementation Plan: `megaplan cloud` — Sprint 1 (provider-agnostic Railway runner)

## Overview

Goal: introduce a new `megaplan/cloud/` subpackage that generalises the working-but-hardcoded Railway deployment at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` into a reusable, spec-driven cloud runner exposed through the existing `megaplan` CLI. Sprint 1 ships only the Railway provider; the abstraction is built so sprint 2 can drop in fly/ssh/local providers without churn.

Repo shape (relevant): the `megaplan` package uses argparse subcommands wired in `megaplan/cli.py` (`build_parser`, `cli.py:826`), with multi-action subcommands defined per-module via `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)` (see `megaplan/chain.py:528` and dispatch at `megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`). Package data is loaded with `importlib.resources.files("megaplan")` (`megaplan/cli.py:600`). Hatchling packages the whole `megaplan/` tree, so any new resource files (Dockerfile, entrypoint, wrappers, templates) ship automatically. PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`).

Hard contracts the plan must respect (verified against the codebase, fixing prior-iteration mistakes):
- **Remote command name**: the deployed image installs `megaplan` globally (reigh `Dockerfile:61-63`); README operations use bare `megaplan` inside `/workspace/<repo>`. The cloud layer must use `megaplan ...`, NOT `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan` — there is no `.venv` in the container.
- **Config namespace**: `megaplan config set` only accepts `agents.<step>` keys, where `<step>` is validated against `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (`megaplan/cli.py:758-777`, `megaplan/types.py:296-309`). There is no `phase_agent.*` namespace. The renderer must emit `agents.<step>` lines and reject unknown step names by importing `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING`.
- **`next_step` translation**: `megaplan/auto.py:128-136` (`_phase_command`) is the canonical translator and adds `--confirm-destructive --user-approved` for `execute`. `cloud resume` must reuse this helper, not stringify `next_step` directly.
- **Active-plan resolution**: `handle_status` (`cli.py:353-367`) calls `load_plan(root, args.plan)` which already auto-resolves when `args.plan is None`. The cloud layer must not duplicate this — `cloud status` and `cloud resume` simply pass `--plan <name>` through if the user provided one and omit the flag otherwise.
- **Railway service/session targeting**: the existing reigh workflow uses `railway --service agent variables --set ...` and `railway ssh --session agent` (per the reigh README). The Railway provider must thread service + session identity through every CLI call, sourced from a `railway:` block in `cloud.yaml`.

Other constraints:
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable.
- Idempotency contract of the boot script preserved (clone-if-missing, codex config always overwritten, megaplan always upgraded).
- Codex `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` retained with explanatory comment.
- `MEGAPLAN_REF` pin surfaced as `megaplan.ref` (default `main`).
- Adding `cloud` must not break any existing command.
- Reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching today's reigh env vars must be able to reproduce the existing deployment.

## Phase 1: Reconcile Source & Scaffold Subpackage

2. **Document** in a brief `megaplan/cloud/_reference/README.md` (or top-of-file comment) that the contents are a frozen reference snapshot, not the runtime templates.

### Step 1: Read every file in the reigh deployment
**Scope:** Small

Do not pre-filter — the brief says "every file" and it means every file.
2. **Catalogue** every hardcoded value (repo URL, workspace path, model, reasoning, email, agent routing, robustness) and every environment variable referenced by `entrypoint.sh`. Map each to a `cloud.yaml` field.
3. **Note** the exact Railway service name (`agent`) and tmux session names (`agent`, `heartbeat`) used throughout the README — these become defaults in the `railway:` block of `cloud.yaml`.
4. **Note** the exact mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredoc bodies — these become standalone files in step 3.

Per user note #3: if no non-interactive path exists, the rendered entrypoint must emit a clear warning and continue (do NOT block deploy). Document the chosen path in `docs/cloud.md`.

### Step 2: Scaffold the subpackage layout (`megaplan/cloud/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the directory tree:
   ```
   megaplan/cloud/
     __init__.py

     spec.py             # cloud.yaml dataclasses + loader/validator
   ```

### Step 3: Port resources verbatim, then parameterise (`megaplan/cloud/templates/`, `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Copy** `Dockerfile`, `healthserver.py`, and `railway.toml` from reigh into `megaplan/cloud/templates/` with no behavioural changes. The Dockerfile bakes the toolchain only; megaplan is installed globally inside the image (preserve the existing `pip install` line so `megaplan` resolves on the container PATH — do NOT switch to a project-local `.venv`).
2. **Copy** `entrypoint.sh` to `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl` and replace every hardcoded value with a Python `string.Template` placeholder (`${REPO_URL}`, `${REPO_BRANCH}`, `${WORKSPACE_PATH}`, `${CODEX_MODEL}`, `${CODEX_REASONING}`, `${CODEX_EMAIL}`, `${MEGAPLAN_REF}`, `${ROBUSTNESS}`, `${MODE}`, `${IDEA_FILE}`, `${CHAIN_SPEC}`, `${AGENT_ROUTING_BLOCK}`, `${CLAUDE_AUTH_BLOCK}`, etc.). Keep the explanatory comment for `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`.

Add a `COPY megaplan/cloud/wrappers/* /usr/local/bin/` (or equivalent) step to the Dockerfile, plus a `chmod +x`. The template no longer carries heredocs.
4. **Render** the Claude auth block as the result of step 1's investigation. Shape:

   ```
   if [ -n "${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:-}" ]; then

   else
       echo "[entrypoint] ANTHROPIC_API_KEY not set; skipping claude auth"
   ```

   If no non-interactive path exists at all, the block becomes a single `echo "[entrypoint] WARNING: claude CLI has no non-interactive auth; tools relying on claude will fail"` — never `exit 1`.

### Step 4: Spec loader (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** dataclasses mirroring the schema. Provider-specific knobs live under a per-provider nested block so the Provider ABC stays clean for sprint 2:
   ```python
   @dataclass
   class RepoSpec: url: str; branch: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class CodexSpec: model: str = "gpt-5.4"; reasoning: str = "high"
   @dataclass
   class AutoSpec: idea_file: str; robustness: str = "standard"
   @dataclass
   class ChainSubSpec: spec: str
   @dataclass
   class ResourcesSpec: volume: str | None = None; port: int = 8080
   @dataclass
   class MegaplanSpec: ref: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class RailwaySpec: service: str = "agent"; session: str = "agent"; project: str | None = None
   @dataclass
   class CloudSpec:
       provider: str                     # "railway" only in sprint 1
       repo: RepoSpec
       agents: dict[str, str]            # keys validated against DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING + "default"
       codex: CodexSpec
       mode: str                         # "auto" | "chain" | "idle"
       megaplan: MegaplanSpec
       resources: ResourcesSpec
       secrets: list[str]
       auto: AutoSpec | None = None
       chain: ChainSubSpec | None = None
       railway: RailwaySpec | None = None  # required when provider == "railway"
   ```
2. **Implement** `load_spec(path: Path) -> CloudSpec` — open, `yaml.safe_load`, validate, raise `CliError("invalid_spec", ...)` on every missing/malformed field. Mirror `megaplan/chain.py:71-100`.
3. **Validate** mode-specific requirements: `mode=auto` ⇒ `auto.idea_file` required; `mode=chain` ⇒ `chain.spec` required; `mode=idle` ⇒ neither required.
4. **Validate** `agents` keys: `default` is permitted; every other key MUST be in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (imported from `megaplan.types`). This eliminates drift — when the routing system grows in `megaplan.types`, cloud.yaml automatically accepts the new step. Reject unknown keys with `CliError("invalid_spec", f"agents.{key} not in DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING: {sorted(DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING)}")`.
5. **Validate** every value in `agents` is in `KNOWN_AGENTS` (also imported from `megaplan.types`).
6. **Validate** `provider`: only `"railway"` is permitted in sprint 1; reject others with `CliError("invalid_spec", "sprint 2 will add: fly, ssh, local")`.
7. **Validate** that when `provider == "railway"`, the `railway:` block exists (use defaults if omitted, but the dataclass instance must be present after load).

### Step 5: Template renderer (`megaplan/cloud/template.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `render_entrypoint(spec: CloudSpec) -> str` using `string.Template.safe_substitute`. Source the `.tmpl` from package resources via `importlib.resources.files("megaplan.cloud").joinpath("templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl").read_text(...)`.
2. **Build** the agent-routing block. For each entry in `spec.agents`:
   - `default` → `megaplan config set agents.<step> <default_agent>` for every `<step>` in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (preserves the "all phases → codex" semantics reigh has today).
   - Per-step keys → `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` (overrides default for that step only).
   The output is a sequence of valid `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` shell lines — no `phase_agent.*`, no fabricated namespace.
3. **Implement** `render_railway_toml(spec)` and `materialize_deploy_dir(spec, dest: Path)` that copies the Dockerfile, healthserver, wrappers, and the rendered entrypoint + railway.toml into a deploy staging dir. The deploy staging dir is what the provider hands to `railway up`.
4. **Single source of truth**: define placeholder names as a module-level constant (e.g. `PLACEHOLDERS = ("REPO_URL", "REPO_BRANCH", ...)`) so tests can iterate over them and assert "no placeholder remains unfilled".

## Phase 2: Provider Layer

### Step 6: Provider abstract base (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Define** an `abc.ABC` `Provider` with the methods sprint-1 commands need. Keep the surface minimal so sprint 2 providers can implement it without leaking Railway-specific concepts:
   ```python
   class Provider(ABC):
       def build(self, deploy_dir: Path) -> int: ...
       def deploy(self, deploy_dir: Path, *, secrets: dict[str, str]) -> int: ...
       def ssh_exec(self, command: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess: ...
       def attach(self) -> int: ...                  # provider decides session/target
       def logs(self, *, follow: bool = True) -> int: ...
       def status_payload(self, *, plan: str | None) -> dict: ...  # remote `megaplan status [--plan ...]`
       def down(self) -> int: ...
       def destroy(self) -> int: ...
   ```
2. **Document** in the docstring that sprint 2 will add `init_plan(self, ...)` and that providers must remain stateless beyond CLI/credential discovery.
3. **Provide** `get_provider(name: str, spec: CloudSpec) -> Provider` so `cli.py` doesn't import each backend directly. Static dict for sprint 1: `{"railway": RailwayProvider}`.

### Step 7: Railway provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Construct** with the `RailwaySpec` (`service`, `session`, optional `project`) so every method targets the right service explicitly.
2. **Implement** each ABC method as a thin wrapper around `railway` CLI:
   - `build`: `docker build -t <image-tag> <deploy_dir>` (pre-Railway local build, optional).
   - `deploy`: render service-targeted `railway up --service <service> --detach --ci` from the deploy dir; preceded by per-secret `railway variables --service <service> --set NAME=VALUE` calls.
   - `ssh_exec(cmd)`: `railway ssh --service <service> --session <session> -- <cmd>` — the session arg matches the existing reigh README.
   - `attach`: `railway ssh --service <service> --session <session>` (interactive). The cloud CLI step accepts `--session` to override the default for `attach` only.
   - `logs`: `railway logs --service <service>` (with `--follow` if requested).
   - `status_payload(plan)`: build remote command as `cd ${WORKSPACE_PATH} && megaplan status` plus `--plan <plan>` if `plan is not None`. Stream stdout, parse JSON, return dict. Use bare `megaplan` (globally installed in the image) — no `.venv`, no `python -m`.
   - `down`: `railway down --service <service>` (or service-targeted equivalent).
   - `destroy`: full teardown using whatever `railway` exposes; the safety prompt lives in the CLI layer, not here.
3. **Detect** the `railway` binary up front via `shutil.which("railway")`; if missing, raise `CliError("provider_unavailable", "Install: https://docs.railway.app/develop/cli")`.
4. **`deploy()` secrets**: source values from `os.environ` for each name in `spec.secrets`. If any are missing locally, raise `CliError("missing_secrets", f"Missing env vars: {missing}")` BEFORE making any railway calls. No silent skip.
5. **No retry/backoff** beyond what `railway` already does. Surface non-zero exits as `CliError("provider_failed", stderr)`.

## Phase 3: CLI Wiring

### Step 8: Cloud CLI module (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` modelled on `megaplan/chain.py:528-555`. Top-level `cloud` parser with subparsers: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. All accept an optional `--cloud-yaml PATH` (defaults to `<root>/cloud.yaml`).
2. **Implement** `run_cloud_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int` that dispatches to per-action functions. Each action loads the spec (except `init`), constructs a provider via `get_provider`, and calls one method.
3. **Per-action behaviours:**
   - **`init`** — copies `cloud.yaml.tmpl` to `<root>/cloud.yaml`. Refuses to overwrite without `--force`.
   - **`build`** — renders deploy dir, calls `provider.build(deploy_dir)`.
   - **`deploy`** — renders deploy dir, gathers secret values from env, calls `provider.deploy(...)`.
   - **`status`** — accepts optional `--plan NAME`; calls `provider.status_payload(plan=args.plan)` and prints the JSON it returns. Auto-discovery happens server-side: when `--plan` is omitted, the remote `megaplan status` is invoked with no `--plan` flag, and `load_plan(root, None)` in `_core` resolves the active plan. No client-side discovery, no extra remote round-trips.
   - **`attach`** — calls `provider.attach()`. `--session NAME` overrides the spec default for this single invocation.
   - **`logs`** — calls `provider.logs(follow=True)`. `--no-follow` for one-shot.
   - **`exec "<cmd>"`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(cmd)`, streams stdout/stderr, returns the remote exit code.
   - **`resume`** — accepts optional `--plan NAME`. Steps:
     1. Call `provider.status_payload(plan=args.plan)`; read `next_step` from the result.
     2. Translate via the local helper: `from megaplan.auto import _phase_command; argv = _phase_command(next_step)`. This guarantees the `execute` special case (`execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved`) is honored — same translator the local `auto` driver uses, so behaviour cannot drift.
     3. Build the remote command: `cd ${WORKSPACE_PATH} && megaplan ` + shell-quoted `argv`, plus `--plan <plan>` if the user specified one.
     4. Hand to `provider.ssh_exec(...)`.
   - **`down`** — calls `provider.down()`.
   - **`destroy`** — interactive confirmation by default; `--yes` skips. (Per user note #5.) On confirmation, calls `provider.destroy()`.
4. **Errors:** all `CliError`s flow through `_emit_error` (mirror `megaplan/chain.py:594-597`) so output is consistent with other megaplan commands.

### Step 9: Wire into top-level CLI (`megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Register** the parser inside `build_parser()` next to the existing `build_chain_parser` registration (`megaplan/cli.py:997-998`):
   ```python
   from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser
   build_cloud_parser(subparsers)
   ```
2. **Dispatch** in `main()` next to the `chain` block (`megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`):
   ```python
   if args.command == "cloud":
       from megaplan.cloud.cli import run_cloud_cli
       try:
           return run_cloud_cli(root, args)
       except CliError as error:
           return error_response(error, root=root)
   ```
3. **Do NOT** add `"cloud"` to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` — it has its own dispatch path, like `auto`/`chain`/`tiebreaker`.
4. **Keep** all imports lazy/scoped to avoid a perf hit on commands that don't touch cloud.

## Phase 4: Tests, Docs, Smoke Build

### Step 10: Unit tests for spec & renderer (`tests/test_cloud_spec.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_cloud_spec.py`** — happy-path load for each `mode`, plus rejections: missing `repo.url`, unknown provider, `mode=auto` without `idea_file`, `mode=chain` without `chain.spec`, an `agents` key that isn't `default` AND isn't in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (assert the error names the actual valid set), an unknown agent value (not in `KNOWN_AGENTS`), malformed `secrets` list, and (for `provider: railway`) a missing `railway:` block being filled with defaults that match reigh (`service: agent`, `session: agent`).
2. **`test_cloud_template.py`** — render with a fully-populated spec and assert: (a) every documented placeholder is replaced (iterate over `template.PLACEHOLDERS`); (b) no original reigh hardcoded string remains (`banodoco/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`); (c) `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` line is preserved; (d) Claude auth block is present and gated on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (see step 3); (e) the agent-routing block emits ONLY `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` lines (no `phase_agent.*`); (f) for a per-step override, exactly one `agents.<step>` line is emitted with the override agent and the rest match the default.

### Step 11: Unit test for the Railway provider with mocked subprocess (`tests/test_cloud_railway.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Patch** `subprocess.run` (and `shutil.which`) and assert exact `railway ...` argv produced by each provider method.
2. **Cover** that every railway invocation includes `--service <service>` from the spec and that `ssh_exec`/`attach` include `--session <session>`.
3. **Cover** `build`, `deploy` (including the per-secret upload sequence with `--service`), `status_payload(plan=None)` and `status_payload(plan="P")` (assert remote command shapes), `down`, `destroy`, `ssh_exec`, `attach`, `logs`.
4. **Assert** the remote command for `status_payload` is `cd <workspace> && megaplan status` (plus `--plan ...` if provided) — no `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan`, no `python -m`.
5. **Cover** the missing-binary path: `shutil.which("railway") -> None` ⇒ `CliError("provider_unavailable", ...)`.
6. **Cover** the missing-secret path: spec lists secret `FOO` but `os.environ` doesn't have it → `CliError` with the missing names.

### Step 12: Unit test for `cloud resume` translation (`tests/test_cloud_resume.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Mock** `provider.status_payload` to return a dict with `next_step == "execute"`. Assert the cloud-resume action calls `provider.ssh_exec` with a remote command containing `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved`. (This is the regression guard for FLAG-003.)
2. **Cover** at least one non-execute step (e.g. `next_step == "review"`) to verify the bare-name path also works.
3. **Assert** that `--plan <name>` is appended when the user passed `--plan`, and omitted when they didn't.

### Step 13: Smoke test for docker build (`tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Skip** at module import if `shutil.which("docker") is None` with a clear `pytest.skip(...)` message.
2. **Materialise** a fully-rendered deploy dir into `tmp_path`, run `docker build -t megaplan-cloud-smoke .` against it. Fail on non-zero exit.
3. **Mark** with `@pytest.mark.slow` so CI can opt in/out.

### Step 14: User docs (`docs/cloud.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/cloud.md` (the directory does not exist yet — create it). Sections: quick start, `cloud.yaml` field reference (including the `railway:` nested block with `service`/`session` and the rationale that other providers will get their own nested block), env vars, secret-rotation pointer, cost-monitoring pointer (Railway dashboard link), the symptom→cause→fix table ported from the reigh README.
2. **Document** the exact non-interactive Claude auth path discovered in step 1 — and explicitly call out the warning-not-fatal behaviour when no path exists.
3. **Document** the `railway` CLI install link and the failure mode if it's missing.
4. **Brief mention** that sprint 2 will add fly/ssh/local providers and `cloud init-plan` — set expectations, do not over-promise.

## Execution Order
1. Steps 0–1 first — vendoring (if needed) and full read of the reigh dir feeds every later decision.
2. Steps 2–3 lay down resources before any code references them.
3. Steps 4–5 (spec + renderer) before steps 6–7 (providers depend on the spec dataclasses).
4. Steps 8–9 (CLI wiring) after the providers exist.
5. Tests (10–13) run alongside or after each implementation step they cover; do not save them all for the end.
6. Docs (14) last so they reflect the shipped behaviour.

## Validation Order
1. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_template.py tests/test_cloud_resume.py -x` (cheapest, no external deps).
2. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_railway.py -x` (mocked subprocess, still cheap).
3. Run the full suite: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -x` to confirm no regression in existing megaplan tests.
4. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud init` in a scratch dir; confirm `cloud.yaml` is written.
5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud build` against the scratch `cloud.yaml`; confirm a Docker image is produced (skip if no docker).
6. Manual: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud deploy` against a throwaway Railway project mirroring reigh's env vars; confirm the container boots, codex authenticates, claude auth either succeeds or warns (never blocks), megaplan installs at the pinned ref, tmux sessions start, healthserver answers on `:8080`. `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud status` returns parsed JSON, `cloud resume` on an `execute`-state plan dispatches `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved` over SSH. Document this run as the parity-validation success criterion artifact.

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-012",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-028",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked step 7 against the brief's explicit prompt-builder requirement. the revision fixes the old context leak by introducing a brand-new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, but the brief said `_review_prompt()` should stay as-is and still run as the `criteria_verdict` check in heavy mode; v3 solves the problem by replacing that heavy-mode criteria path instead of reusing the baseline review prompt, which is still a material divergence from the requested design.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the prior missing locations in `megaplan/workers.py` and `tests/test_workers.py` are now covered, but one supporting-infrastructure path is still absent from the steps: the repo-root generated schema copy under `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and the tests that read it directly. without either explicitly regenerating that file or changing those tests to materialize schemas in a temp root, the plan can still leave the runtime-schema copy out of sync with the raw registry.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-megaplan-s-planning-20260409-2103"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "execute-phase-contract": [

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i rechecked the caller paths after the revision. the raw-schema caller path through `validate_payload()` is now accounted for, but the runtime-schema caller path still is not: `strict_schema()` only reaches the on-disk runtime schemas through `ensure_runtime_layout()`, while current tests like `tests/test_schemas.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` consume the already-written repo copy directly. the plan does not yet say how that caller path gets refreshed or asserted after the code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-009",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-010",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-029",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-v2"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-032",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-046",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        Idea:
Build `megaplan cloud` — a new subpackage at `megaplan/cloud/` that provides a built-in, provider-agnostic cloud runner wired into the existing megaplan CLI. This is sprint 1 of a two-sprint effort; the goal here is operational parity with the existing Railway setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`, nothing more.

### What exists today (to be generalised)
The folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:
- `Dockerfile` — Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git
- `entrypoint.sh` — idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases→codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver
- `healthserver.py` — tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck
- `railway.toml` — build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy
- `chain.sh` — thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`
- `chain.yaml.example` — chain spec format
- `README.md` — recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands

Hardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases→codex routing, `standard` robustness. Bug to fix: Claude Code CLI is installed in the image but the entrypoint never authenticates it — add an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auth path.

### Sprint 1 deliverables
1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:
   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.
   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency — confirm/add.
   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.
   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this — `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).
   - Railway provider plugin: thin wrapper over `railway` CLI (`up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables --set`, `logs`, `status`). Live at `megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py` with a `Provider` abstract base at `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py` ready for sprint-2 plugins.

2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:
   - `megaplan cloud init` — scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root
   - `megaplan cloud build` — docker build the image
   - `megaplan cloud deploy` — render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`
   - `megaplan cloud status` — invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser — reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)
   - `megaplan cloud attach` — attach to tmux `agent` session
   - `megaplan cloud logs` — tail remote logs
   - `megaplan cloud exec "<cmd>"` — run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)
   - `megaplan cloud resume` — read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`
   - `megaplan cloud down` — pause without deleting volume
   - `megaplan cloud destroy` — full teardown

3. Documentation at `docs/cloud.md`: env-var / cloud.yaml reference, symptom→cause→fix table (port from existing README), secret-rotation and cost-monitoring pointers.

4. Tests:
   - Unit: spec loader (happy path + validation errors)
   - Unit: template renderer (all hardcoded strings correctly replaced)
   - Unit: Railway provider with mocked subprocess
   - Smoke: `docker build` on the generated Dockerfile must succeed (CI-runnable; can be skipped on systems without docker with a clear warning)

5. Reigh parity validation (success criterion, not a test to automate): after implementation, the existing `reigh-megaplan-dev` behavior can be reproduced by `megaplan cloud deploy` with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars. The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.

### Explicit non-goals for sprint 1
- Do NOT ship fly / ssh / local providers — that is sprint 2.
- Do NOT ship `cloud init-plan` bootstrap — sprint 2.
- Do NOT add a `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` — sprint 2.
- Do NOT retire `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` — it keeps running in parallel until sprint 2 migrates reigh fully.

### Constraints (hard requirements)
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable: if sprint 2 slips, this release is still useful on its own.
- Preserve the idempotency contract of the current entrypoint (repo clone only if missing; codex config always overwritten on boot; megaplan always upgraded on boot).
- Preserve `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` in the generated codex config with an explanatory comment — the container IS the sandbox.
- Preserve the `MEGAPLAN_REF` pinning mechanism, now surfaced as `megaplan.ref` in `cloud.yaml` (defaults to `main`).
- `railway` CLI is an acceptable runtime dependency; document it and fail gracefully with a link if missing.
- Do not break existing megaplan commands — `cloud` is additive.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: `megaplan cloud` — Sprint 1 (provider-agnostic Railway runner)

## Overview

Goal: introduce a new `megaplan/cloud/` subpackage that generalises the working-but-hardcoded Railway deployment at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` into a reusable, spec-driven cloud runner exposed through the existing `megaplan` CLI. Sprint 1 ships only the Railway provider; the abstraction is built so sprint 2 can drop in fly/ssh/local providers without churn.

Repo shape (relevant): the `megaplan` package uses argparse subcommands wired in `megaplan/cli.py` (`build_parser`, `cli.py:826`), with multi-action subcommands defined per-module via `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)` (see `megaplan/chain.py:528` and dispatch at `megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`). Package data is loaded with `importlib.resources.files("megaplan")` (`megaplan/cli.py:600`). Hatchling packages the whole `megaplan/` tree, so any new resource files (Dockerfile, entrypoint, wrappers, templates) ship automatically. PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`). Existing `.gitignore` already lists `__pycache__/`, `.megaplan/`, `dist/`, `*.egg-info/`, `activity-summary-*.md` (no rule for `_reference/` yet — Step 0 adds one).

Hard contracts the plan must respect (verified against the codebase, fixing prior-iteration mistakes):
- **Remote command name**: the deployed image installs `megaplan` globally (reigh `Dockerfile:61-63`); README operations use bare `megaplan` inside `/workspace/<repo>`. The cloud layer must use `megaplan ...`, NOT `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan` — there is no `.venv` in the container.
- **Config namespace**: `megaplan config set` only accepts `agents.<step>` keys, where `<step>` is validated against `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (`megaplan/cli.py:758-777`, `megaplan/types.py:296-309`). There is no `phase_agent.*` namespace.
- **`next_step` translation**: `megaplan/auto.py:128-136` (`_phase_command`) is the canonical translator and adds `--confirm-destructive --user-approved` for `execute`. `cloud resume` must reuse this helper.
- **Active-plan resolution**: `handle_status` (`cli.py:353-367`) calls `load_plan(root, args.plan)` which already auto-resolves when `args.plan is None`. The cloud layer passes `--plan` through if the user provided one and omits it otherwise — no client-side discovery.
- **Railway service/session targeting**: existing reigh workflow uses `railway --service agent variables --set ...` and `railway ssh --session agent`. The Railway provider threads service + session identity through every CLI call.
- **Workspace path is data, not magic**: reigh hardcodes `/workspace/reigh-app` in five places (`safe.directory`, `REPO_DIR`, tmux `-c`, README `cd` invocations). The spec MUST carry this path as a first-class field; templates and remote commands MUST read it from there.
- **Volume teardown is part of `destroy`**: reigh README defines full teardown as `railway down` + `railway volume delete <volume>`. `Provider.destroy(*, volume=None)` and `RailwayProvider` MUST honor a configured volume name.
- **No dead knobs**: `railway.project` is wired (called once at deploy via `railway link --project <project>` when set) — not added speculatively.

Other constraints:
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable.
- Idempotency contract preserved (clone-if-missing, codex config always overwritten, megaplan always upgraded).
- Codex `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` retained with explanatory comment.
- `MEGAPLAN_REF` pin surfaced as `megaplan.ref` (default `main`).
- Adding `cloud` must not break any existing command.
- Reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching today's reigh env vars must reproduce the existing deployment.

## Phase 1: Reconcile Source & Scaffold Subpackage

### Step 0: Vendor reigh source as reference, with ignore + build exclusion (`megaplan/cloud/_reference/`, `.gitignore`, `pyproject.toml`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** the executor can read `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`. If readable, proceed; if sandboxed, copy the entire directory tree into `megaplan/cloud/_reference/` so subsequent steps have ground truth.
2. **Append** the line `megaplan/cloud/_reference/` to `.gitignore` (the file currently has 9 lines; add at the bottom). This prevents the snapshot from being committed if the vendoring fallback is used.
3. **Exclude** the snapshot from packaged artifacts: add `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.exclude] packages = ["megaplan/cloud/_reference"]` (or the equivalent `exclude = ["megaplan/cloud/_reference/**"]`) to `pyproject.toml`. Verify by inspecting the built wheel — `_reference/` must NOT appear.
4. **Document** the snapshot status in `megaplan/cloud/_reference/README.md` (one paragraph: "frozen reference from reigh-megaplan-dev as of <date>; do not edit; not shipped").

### Step 1: Read every file in the reigh deployment
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** every file in `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` (or the vendored mirror): `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`, `healthserver.py`, `railway.toml`, `chain.sh`, `chain.yaml.example`, `README.md`, `BRIEFING_RULES.md`, every file under `ideas/`, every top-level `megaplan-idea*.txt`, plus anything else present.
2. **Catalogue** every hardcoded value (repo URL, **workspace path** (`/workspace/reigh-app`), model, reasoning, email, agent routing, robustness, **persistent volume name** (`agent-volume`)) and every environment variable referenced by `entrypoint.sh`.
3. **Confirm** the exact volume name reigh uses (per README: `agent-volume`) and the Railway service/session names (`agent`/`agent`) — these become defaults in the `railway:` block of `cloud.yaml`.
4. **Note** the exact mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredoc bodies — these become standalone files in step 3.
5. **Identify** the exact `claude` CLI auth invocation. Test what the installed `claude` CLI exposes for non-interactive auth. Per user note #3: if no non-interactive path exists, the rendered entrypoint emits a warning and continues (do NOT block deploy).

### Step 2: Scaffold the subpackage layout (`megaplan/cloud/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the directory tree:
   ```
   megaplan/cloud/
     __init__.py
     cli.py              # build_cloud_parser + run_cloud_cli
     spec.py             # cloud.yaml dataclasses + loader/validator
     template.py         # entrypoint renderer

       mp-supervise
   ```
